# Task ledger

States are `TODO`, `ACTIVE`, `BLOCKED`, and `DONE`. A task is `DONE` only when
its listed evidence is committed or reproducibly generated by a committed
command. Task IDs are stable.

## M0: Repository and proof design

| ID | State | Task | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0-01 | DONE | Pin oracle, stable target, and version ladder | Revisions and hashes documented |
| M0-02 | DONE | Define architecture and compatibility policy | Architecture review finds no layer cycles |
| M0-03 | DONE | Identify live-bootstrap insertion point | Exact manifest stage and proof command recorded |
| M0-04 | DONE | Fast-forward oracle baseline and record delta | Branch contains upstream remote HEAD or explicit pin rationale |
| M0-05 | DONE | Establish build/test entry points | One command runs development and TCC gates |

| M1-03 | DONE | Strings, byte buffers, typed vectors | Unit and fault-injection tests pass |

| M1-07 | ACTIVE | TCC ABI/runtime probe suite | Required probes classified pass/emulated/blocked |

### Semantic barrier checkpoints

- The complete cfg-active body manifest now advances atomically through
  `STRUCTURAL -> TYPED -> MARKED -> REGIONS`. MARKED computes the bounded C
  expression slice's value usage with its builtin-Copy subset and writes a
  NOT_PROMOTED sentinel because that slice admits no promotion-ready borrow
  form. REGIONS is a read-only structural closure proof over represented
  parameter/result/value types, body locals, expression types, direct and
  selected-call arguments, substitutions, generic arguments/defaults,
  predicate subjects/bounds/equality terms, outlives terms, const-argument
  types, and their conservatively bounded nested type forms. It accepts
  static/erased regions, exact early-bound lifetime parameters owned by the
  body item or its authenticated enclosing trait/impl, and exact late-bound
  indices owned by the active predicate binder. It rejects inference/error
  regions, inference/error types and consts, wrong parameter or binder
  ownership, cycles, shared DAGs, wrong body ownership, malformed slices, and
  inconsistent MARKED evidence. Success changes no HIR generation and mints a
  fresh REGIONS capability; every failure preserves phase, generation, and
  capability.
- This first REGIONS checkpoint is deliberately narrower than original
  mrustc's lifetime solver. Predicate traversal proves only structural closure
  and exact scoping; it does not solve traits, equality, or outlives facts. It
  does not yet traverse supertraits, associated bounds, ADT field declarations,
  enum discriminants, or type-position expressions. It does not claim lifetime
  inference, outlives/equality solving, promotion eligibility, or borrow
  semantics.
  Enum discriminants and unevaluated array lengths remain outside the body
  manifest until they have stable type-position atom identities. The next
  checkpoint begins the ordered closure/static-promotion/vtable/UFCS/reborrow/
  erased-type rewrites before independent rewritten-HIR validation.

### Active front-end evidence

The active states above are intentional: the committed foundations do not yet
meet the corpus-wide acceptance gates.

- M2-04 has structural `cfg`/`cfg_attr` views, builtin expansion and reparsing,
  cfg-active declaration lowering, and source-qualified cfg-active module item
  views (`141e7397`, `105c10fd`, `1f97e5b5`, `bdb42acc`, `784ee526`). The
  graph now borrows one explicit cfg set as the authority for every source
  unit and reapplies cfg/cfg_attr to every generated item sequence before
  recursive item-macro planning. Effective attribute snapshots store metadata
  bodies without source wrappers. This is focused evidence only; the
  Rust-core cfg acceptance corpus remains.
  The compiler driver now derives intrinsic cfg facts from the exact canonical
  target descriptor, including architecture, OS, environment, ABI, vendor,
  family, pointer width, endianness, baseline target features, thread-local
  availability, and target atomic classes. A caller-built descriptor must be
  byte-for-byte equivalent in semantic fields to the registered descriptor for
  its triple. Rust does not expose edition cfg facts, so the former invented
  `edition = "..."` and `rust_20xx` predicates were removed. Crate features,
  command-line cfg values, panic mode, sanitizers, and codegen options remain
  unowned and are not fabricated.
- M2-05 has bounded parsing/matching/transcription, explicit macro AST nodes,
  wrapper-free output reparsing, and deterministic lexical item-macro planning
  (`b8c61414`, `49c8de1d`, `9e5b5cef`, `a51e8b99`, `5ab1212f`, `ba6f4990`,
  `537a9733`). Planner nodes now own effective attributes, the outermost source
  invocation anchor, and source-qualified immediate generated-item invocation,
  definition, and depth provenance. Every parsed graph source unit owns one
  contextual plan; external source files inherit the parent textual
  `macro_rules!` scope captured at their declaring `mod`, while preserving
  declaration order, cfg filtering, nested propagation, and local shadowing.
  Generated declarations enter namespaces and generated inline modules recurse
  through the same graph builder. The planner now accepts bounded exact
  source-qualified invocation-to-declaration records, validates each target
  against its declaration AST, and consumes every record exactly once. Those
  records can authorize aliased, imported, and qualified paths without
  deriving identity from spelling; duplicate and inactive/stale records reject
  atomically. An explicit skeleton mode records
  unresolved source invocations as exact pending identities while omitting
  them from semantic roots; local forward references and unresolved generated
  invocations still reject. The production graph now uses that mode to build
  cfg-active modules and complete declaration histories, including inherited
  textual scope; resolves each unqualified pending call at its exact lexical
  position; replans with the exact declaration identities; discards the
  skeleton's derived storage; and publishes only the rebuilt graph. A
  cross-source fixture proves local-to-`macro_use`-to-local shadow ordering and
  exact expansion provenance. Staging now repeats transactionally when a full
  rebuild discovers pending calls in newly loaded source modules. Source calls
  inside traits and impls can be retained during discovery with their nearest
  module container, but final expansion there remains unsupported. Public
  source-qualified local-crate paths now resolve through the same fixed-point
  module/import model and pass one exact declaration identity back to the
  planner. Private, ambiguous, unresolved, extern-prelude, and generated
  qualified paths remain fail-closed; unqualified `use`-imported invocation
  lookup is still unsupported. Hygiene,
  expression-position expansion,
  proc macros, and the Rust-core macro acceptance corpus remain unsupported or
  unproven as applicable.
  The syntax layer now distinguishes invocations, `macro_rules!` definitions,
  and declarative `pub macro` definitions. Both Rust 1.90 declaration forms
  preserve visibility, attributes, name, optional parameter token tree, body,
  delimiter, and span. All 19 declarative macros in the pinned
  `core/src/macros/mod.rs` parse. Rule-bodied definitions without a parameter
  list now use the bounded `macro_rules!` matcher/transcriber, while
  parameter-style declarations still reject explicitly by default. The one
  admitted exception is the exact resolver-certified compiler builtin
  `cfg_select`: its cfg-active declaration must have exactly one bare
  `rustc_builtin_macro` attribute, source calls select the first true predicate
  or final `_` fallback, and only the selected item group is reparsed.
  Shadowed, similarly named, malformed, cfg-disabled, and generated calls fail
  closed. Item-macro plans separately retain
  every cfg-active named macro declaration, its effective attributes,
  immediate inline-module container, form, span, and generated provenance.
  Cfg-disabled declarations do not enter that list. This preserves the exact
  `rustc_builtin_macro` and `macro_export` metadata needed by the next resolver
  slice without exposing definitions as ordinary semantic roots.
  Function-item qualification now recognizes `const` followed by the remaining
  `async`, `unsafe`, and `extern` qualifiers before `fn`, rather than
  misclassifying forms such as `pub const unsafe fn` as const items. Array

  The impl parser also distinguishes the rustdoc primitive placeholder

  normalized into the same relaxed-sized parameter property as inline
  `T: ?Sized`, without inventing a trait predicate. This clears
  `atomic_cxchg` at `core/src/intrinsics/mod.rs:96`, including both
  `AtomicOrdering` parameters. Inline lifetime bounds on type and lifetime
  generic parameters now lower as ordered item outlives predicates, retaining
  exact bound spans and early-bound or `'static` region identity. This clears
  `T: ?Sized + 'static` in `type_id` at
  `core/src/intrinsics/mod.rs:2757`. The const-default slice clears
  `{ Assume::NOTHING }` on `ASSUME: Assume` in `TransmuteFrom` at
  `core/src/mem/transmutability.rs:90`. The canonical 363-source/451-module
  probe still has zero graph/import errors. Lifetime-generic trait methods now
  retain method-owned early-bound regions in receivers, arguments, and default
  bodies, clearing `Error::provide<'a>` at `core/src/error.rs:204`.
  Trait-owned lifetime parameters and lifetime supertraits lower structurally

  `core/src/error.rs:971`. Trailing type defaults are now retained on structs,
  unions, and enums as well as aliases and traits. ADT applications fill
  omitted trailing type arguments from primitive defaults or from a prior

  `core/src/ops/control_flow.rs:87`. The canonical GCC- and TinyCC-built probes

  `SpecArrayClone::clone` in `core/src/array/mod.rs:454`. Array types such as
  `[Self; N]` retain `N` as a typed `CM_HIR_CONST_PARAMETER`; non-const or
  non-`usize` array-length parameters and function generic defaults remain

  now enter a transaction-marked registry before trait defaults lower, without

  admits the source-order-independent default
  `FromResidual<R = <Self as Try>::Residual>` while retaining wrong-kind,
  arity, duplicate, bounded/GAT, and rollback rejection. Defaulted supertraits
  continue to see their completed defaults before trait headers bind. The
  canonical GCC- and TinyCC-built probes still report zero graph/import errors
  over 363 sources and 451 modules. The resolver now authenticates the single
  cfg-active private root glob carrying the exact effective
  `#[prelude_import]` attribute and retains its resolved public bindings as a
  lowest-priority fallback for unqualified lookup in every module. Direct and
  imported bindings shadow that fallback; qualified, private, malformed,
  duplicate, nested, public, ambiguous, and non-glob cases remain fail-closed.
  This clears the `Sized` supertrait on `FullOps` at
  `core/src/num/bignum.rs:23`. Ordered positional supertrait arguments and
  associated equalities now retain their resolved trait/associated DefIds,

  `ops::Shr<u32, Output = Self>` at
  `core/src/num/dec2flt/float.rs:20`. Literal `'static` supertraits lower as
  structural `Self: 'static` predicates, while free, inferred, and wrong-kind
  lifetime bounds reject. Trait aliases now retain ordered trait and lifetime
  RHS bounds, generic arguments, associated equalities, modifiers, exact
  spans, and alias-owned generic defaults. Alias references are admitted in
  predicates and supertraits, mixed trait/alias cycles reject iteratively, and
  impl headers cannot name an alias. This clears `Thin` in
  `core/src/ptr/metadata.rs:84`. Auto traits now retain authenticated identity,

  positive/negative overlap reject. Structural trait type defaults recursively
  substitute `Self` plus prior lifetime/type arguments with DAG memoization,
  while explicit static, early-bound, and predicate-binder lifetime arguments
  retain their authenticated region identity. Const trait arguments remain
  unsupported and no source-reachable const-default claim is made.
  Associated-type trait bounds now retain positional lifetime/type arguments
  and Self-free trailing defaults separately from associated equalities.
  Lifetime bounds lower as exact projection-subject outlives predicates with
  authenticated static or enclosing early-bound regions; inferred/free,
  wrong-owner, binderless-late, and GAT cases fail closed. Trait defaults such
  as `PartialEq<Rhs = Self>` now substitute the bounded associated projection

  (`item=0`, span `1247..1259`), where inherited `Self::Output` must retain the
  defining generic supertrait's arguments.
  Each source
  `use` is retained
  in its module as a structural record containing the raw
  tree, declared visibility, effective attributes, and resolved namespace
  bindings; it is not a definition or synthetic HIR item. Import retention
  alone does not implement downstream lookup,
  visibility enforcement, or anonymous-trait scope. Value/body path
  resolution, extern-prelude resolution, expression lowering, and canonical
  HIR oracle parity remain. Before Rust-core corpus scaling,
  per-path segment allocation and the linear source/item declaration lookup
  should be replaced by reusable scratch storage and a keyed index.
- M3-03 has a first bounded type-alias substitution slice. Definitions and all
  lifetime/type generic parameters are predeclared before targets lower; alias
  applications remain explicit transient HIR nodes until every declaration is
  bound, then structural normalization substitutes parameters through
  references, pointers, tuples, arrays, slices, function pointers, and nominal
  arguments without changing nominal DefIds. Forward chains, imports, chained
  reexports, macro-generated generic aliases, inferred lifetimes, distinct
  concrete instantiations, and nested non-cycles have focused coverage. Wrong
  arity/kind and direct, structural, generic, or cross-module cycles hard-error,
  and both the normalizer and graph transaction rewind their allocations on
  failure. Trailing type-parameter defaults on free aliases are now structural
  from parsing through HIR: defaults can refer to earlier lifetime/type
  parameters, instantiate sequentially after explicit arguments, and remain
  overrideable. Omitted and partial defaults, forward/default alias chains,
  imported/chained reexports, generated aliases, explicit or inferred leading
  lifetimes, unused malformed signatures, cycles through defaults, direct API
  rollback, and graph transaction rollback have focused coverage. The public
  HIR model assigns a default only while its item definition is reserved,
  rejects self/later/foreign parameter dependencies, and prevents bypassing the
  setter. Lifetime defaults and type or const defaults on functions/impls are
  parser errors; otherwise-valid type defaults on structs and enums, plus
  literal, compound, and non-path const defaults, remain explicit hard errors
  in HIR. A first explicit projection slice now preserves
  definition-backed `<T as Trait>::Assoc` types from parsing through HIR and
  alias substitution, including forward declarations, use in trailing alias
  defaults, local module paths, imports, and chained reexports. Traits enter the
  type namespace and own bare, targetless associated type declarations without
  publishing those children in their module namespace. The ordered nominal
  generic impl checkpoint lowers local, positive impls of local, nongeneric
  traits for a scalar or local struct/enum self type. Its generic shape is
  exactly `impl<T, U, ...> Trait for LocalNominal<T, U, ...>`: impl parameters
  are type-only and have no defaults, their count equals the nominal arity, and
  the self arguments use each parameter exactly once in declaration order.
  Monomorphic scalar and zero-argument local nominal impls remain supported.
  Impl children may contain bare associated type definitions and ordinary
  non-generic Rust-ABI methods. They must define every required associated
  type and method exactly once unless a trait method has a default body, and
  must match the trait's safety. Impl methods record the corresponding trait
  method DefId; trait defaults may be omitted or overridden. Receivers remain
  parameter zero and are classified as value, shared reference, mutable
  reference, custom typed, or absent. `mut self` produces a mutable local,
  while `&mut self` produces an immutable binding whose reference pointee is
  mutable. Typed receivers require a plain `self` binding. Symbolic `Self` is
  owned by the containing trait/impl and survives alias normalization and
  generic instantiation; projection selection conservatively returns
  `DEFERRED_SELF` when symbolic `Self` is queried. Allocation-free
  `cm_hir_match_projection` reports the matching template and identities;
  mutable `cm_hir_select_projection` instantiates it.
  A direct target such as `type Assoc = T` reuses the query argument TypeId
  without allocation, while structural substitution appends replacement types
  without mutating existing HIR and rewinds those additions on failure.
  Unsupported candidates that may apply conservatively return
  `DEFERRED_ARGUMENTS` and are not skipped in favor of another candidate;
  multiple exact candidates return `AMBIGUOUS`, and substitution failure never
  publishes a partial target or identities. Both APIs take the requesting
  crate explicitly: foreign traits or nominal self types and potentially
  matching foreign impls return `DEFERRED_CRATE`, while unrelated foreign impls
  do not block an exact local selection. Repeated, reordered, nested, fixed,
  omitted, or unused impl parameters remain unsupported, as do predicates and
  where clauses, specialization, defaults, lifetime/const impl generics,
  generic traits, trait or associated arguments, GATs, blanket impls,
  cross-crate selection, alias normalization during matching, and recursive
  projection selection. Method bodies are retained as unlowered
  `(source, expression-id)` identities; this checkpoint does not type-check or
  execute them and does not implement method lookup or dispatch. Source-backed
  graph lowering preserves effective outer method/item attributes as ordered
  structural metadata, including metadata bodies, source attribute identity,
  span, and expansion depth. Graph attributes also expose an explicit owner;
  a HIR attribute's owner is its containing item. Raw lowering still rejects
  attributed associated items, and standalone expanded lowering still rejects
  attributed associated types. Attribute preservation is not semantic handling:
  consumers must
  implement effectful attributes such as `track_caller` or hard-error. Graph
  completeness is checked over only the cfg-active recursive child set, so a
  disabled required trait method does not require an impl definition; a
  missing active required method still fails the transaction. Bounds and
  predicates, method generics/where clauses, non-Rust ABI methods, const/async
  methods, trait associated consts, inherent impl features beyond safe positive
  headers and bounded const definitions, semantic use of retained negative
  impls, shorthand `T::Assoc`,
  `<T>::Assoc`, expression UFCS,
  associated bindings, anonymous trait imports, and unsupported body-bearing
  graph root shapes also remain explicit errors or open work as applicable.
  The pinned
  Rust 1.90 `core`/`alloc` census has 407 explicit associated-type impls, 385
  method-bearing. Fifty-eight fit a broader nominal header funnel; the exact
  parser-compatible ordered type-only boundary leaves 14 non-`impl const`
  forms, all 14 method-bearing with 21 methods total. Twelve impl parents have
  attributes (ten ordinary and two cfg-only); 13 methods across eight impls
  have ordinary `inline` attributes, so 12 of 14 impls require ordinary
  attributes somewhere while the other two are cfg-only. This exact set has no
  cfg_attr or generated impl/method occurrences. Two methods have typed
  receivers and 19 have simple non-self parameter shapes; all 21 are
  non-generic, safe, non-const, non-async, non-extern, non-variadic, and have no
  method where clause. Four signatures contain a supported single
  `Self::Assoc` projection. The trait grouping is `Future` 2/2,
  `Iterator` 5/10, `Try` 2/4, `IntoIterator` 2/2, `Deref` 1/1, and
  `AsVecIntoIter` 2/2 (impls/methods). All require their trait definition to be
  active and lowered. `AsVecIntoIter` was the first compact compatible
  definition. An extracted three-file fixture from
  `library/alloc/src/vec/in_place_collect.rs:428` and
  `library/alloc/src/vec/into_iter.rs:531` now proves its unsafe trait/impl
  safety, sibling-module reexports, ordered generic substitution,
  `Self::Item` projection, mutable receiver, body provenance, and
  `cfg(not(no_global_oom_handling))` disappearance together. The fixture
  intentionally simplifies the surrounding `IntoIter` definition, but now
  retains upstream `#[stable]` on its public reexport. Its public and
  `pub(crate)` import visibility is structural HIR metadata, not evidence that
  HIR consumers enforce import accessibility. It has one associated type and
  one attribute-free method; the binary-heap impl
  at `library/alloc/src/collections/binary_heap/mod.rs:1680` under
  `cfg(not(test))` is its sibling. A second extracted fixture admits the exact
  Rust 1.90 `Future` declaration and `Ready<T>` impl signature. Custom receiver
  types may now follow references and single-argument nominal wrappers to the
  enclosing `Self`, so `mut self: Pin<&mut Self>` retains both its custom
  receiver shape and mutable body local. Alias normalization rechecks that
  root; an unrelated or alias-erased receiver hard-errors. This is a
  structural precondition, not proof of the eventual `Receiver` trait
  obligation. The fixture preserves upstream trait/associated/method/impl
  attributes and source provenance while intentionally minimizing surrounding
  definitions and the method body. The same fixture now admits the exact
  `Pending<T>` impl signature and `PhantomData<fn() -> T>` field. Bare `_`
  parameters are explicit nameless signature discards and do not create body
  locals; each real parameter local records its original signature index, so
  `Future::poll`'s `cx` and `Ready::poll`'s `_cx` remain named while
  `Pending::poll` has two signature positions and only the `self` body local.
  A third extracted fixture admits the exact Rust 1.90 `Try` declaration,
  including its ordered `~const FromResidual` supertrait edge, plus the
  `ControlFlow<B, C>` and `NeverShortCircuit<T>` `Try` impl signatures. It
  deliberately replaces the surrounding generic
  `FromResidual<R = <Self as Try>::Residual>` declaration with a nongeneric
  empty trait: this proves the selected `Try` declaration and impl headers,
  not generic-trait defaults. Structured trait supertraits are retained in
  source order as locally resolved named trait types with their bound spans
  and an exact required-versus-`~const` modifier. Reserved definitions support
  forward edges. HIR insertion prevents a reserved target from later binding
  as a non-trait or with an incompatible generic signature and rejects an edge
  that completes a cycle; final lowering also rejects unresolved/non-trait
  targets and duplicate direct edges. Reachability and final graph validation
  use explicit work stacks rather than recursion.
  A fourth extracted fixture admits the exact Rust 1.90 `IntoIterator`
  declaration, including the ordered required `Iterator` bound on `IntoIter`
  and its resolved `Item = Self::Item` equality, and the exact `Deref`
  declaration, including its `PointeeSized` supertrait and relaxed
  `type Target: ?Sized` bound. Associated-type bounds and equality bindings
  retain source order, spans, resolved trait/associated identities, and the
  enclosing trait as the `Self` owner. Only local nongeneric bound traits,
  nongeneric type equalities, and the exact relaxed `?Sized` form are admitted;
  defaults, GATs, positional arguments, other relaxed bounds, and semantic
  obligation solving hard-error. Canonical dumps expose supertraits and these
  records as `hir-v15`. This is structural representation only: supertrait or
  associated-bound obligations, conditional-const selection, unsizing, and
  the complete upstream `try_trait.rs` are not implemented or claimed. The
  omitted generic `FromResidual` declaration is now admitted by registering
  targetless associated types before trait generic defaults while preserving
  later projection, cycle, and transactional rollback validation. The fifth
  extracted fixture preserves the exact Rust 1.90 attributes and
  signatures of 68 `Iterator` methods. Trait and trait-method type parameters,
  ordered inline-plus-where predicates, positional trait arguments,
  associated equalities, shorthand `T::Assoc`, generic supertrait arguments,
  and trailing type defaults such as `PartialOrd<Rhs = Self>` are structural.
  Callable notation canonicalizes `FnMut(A, B) -> R` to
  `FnMut<(A, B), Output = R>`; a parser-only provenance marker admits its
  one-input tuple without broadening the deliberately ambiguous `(T)`/`(T,)`
  source-tuple slice. Predicate equalities retain resolved inherited associated
  definitions, values, order, and exact spans in deterministic `hir-v15` dumps.
  Required versus default body presence is retained, while default bodies are
  deliberately minimized because expression lowering is not claimed. The nine
  excluded methods are `next_chunk`, `map_windows`, `array_chunks`,
  `partition_in_place`, `copied`, `cloned`, `try_collect`, `try_reduce`, and
  `try_find`; they require const/lifetime method generics or nested associated
  constraints and `impl Trait`. Predicate storage does not implement obligation
  solving, method applicability, or dispatch. These are declaration and
  graph-admission facts, not evidence of expression semantics,
  method lookup/dispatch, code generation, or selection.
  M4-02 remains `ACTIVE`, and this focused checkpoint does not satisfy its
  selected-impl oracle gate or the Rust-core acceptance gate.
- M3-02 now also has parser and model evidence for named struct expressions.
  Explicit, shorthand, empty, and final `..base` syntax is retained, with
  control-head brace disambiguation and deterministic update-base dumps.
  Unsafe blocks retain an explicit AST marker instead of being stolen as a
  keyword-named struct. A
  byte-identical Rust 1.90 `library/core/src/future/pending.rs` fixture parses.
  HIR `hir-v15` structurally authenticates complete constructions of bound
  local nongeneric named structs: every source-ordered field carries its exact
  declaration ordinal, typed body-owned value, and nested span; insertion
  validates complete unique coverage before allocation and deep-copies the
  records. Source-qualified body lowering now accepts those same complete
  local constructions, authenticates direct, qualified, and imported-alias
  paths against the exact resolver/graph/module-map revision, enforces item
  and field visibility, and recursively types heterogeneous or nested field
  values. Reordered fields remain in source order; shorthand and empty named
  structs preserve exact spans. Calls and aggregate fields share one
  preflight-sized transaction allocation with exactly one owner, and stale
  resolver snapshots, malformed coverage, unsupported update/generic/private
  construction, or allocation failure publish no body, local, expression,
  arena, or interner mutation. Named `.field` syntax retains exact base, name,
  and full spans while numeric tuple projection rejects explicitly. Graph HIR
  materializes a nominal self type for every local nongeneric named struct;
  body lowering uses it to authenticate field reads from parameters, fresh
  reordered constructions, and nested projection chains. Each `FIELD` node
  records the exact base, aggregate DefId, declaration ordinal, and declared
  result type. Wrong base/type/name/visibility, stale resolver identity, and
  cyclic syntax reject transactionally. Struct update, generic or cross-crate
  construction, MIR places, and code generation remain explicit open work.
  A checked layout query now covers i32/u32 leaves and recursively nested
  same-crate local nongeneric named structs. It computes declaration-order
  offsets under explicit 32- and 64-bit address models with checked arithmetic
  and atomic outputs, while empty, generic, tuple, union, represented,
  recursive, cross-crate, unsupported-leaf, malformed, or capacity-limited
  requests reject. Strict GCC, TinyCC 0.9.27, and focused ASan/UBSan/LSan gates
  pass. This is not aggregate ABI or MIR/codegen admission and does not close
  the graph-wide M3-02 or M5-04 gates.
  A strict whole-file census over every `.rs` file below pinned Rust 1.90
  `library/core` and `library/alloc` (334 files, without cfg or test pruning)
  moves from 23 successes at `b3e04694` to 41, with no previously successful
  file regressing. This is parse acceptance only, not graph or semantic
  admission.

The current front-end checkpoint passes `make -B -C cmrustc check-all` under
strict C99 GCC and TinyCC 0.9.27. The same complete test target passes GCC
ASan/UBSan with leak detection enabled. These gates establish regression and
portability evidence only; they do not close any corpus-wide milestone above.

The first bounded executable checkpoint crosses the previously absent semantic
pipeline with one deliberately exact program shape:

```text
source graph -> declaration HIR -> typed integer/block HIR -> MIR
             -> portable C -> TinyCC executable -> observed exit status
```

The accepted crate has exactly the three `feature(no_core)`, `no_core`, and
`no_main` inner attributes and exactly one public, nongeneric, safe,
non-variadic `#[no_mangle] extern "C" fn main() -> i32`. Its body is one
suffixed in-range `i32` literal. Body lowering authenticates the exact graph
revision and HIR owner through the module map, then retains source-qualified
block and integer expressions; MIR contains one return local, one constant
assignment, and `RETURN`; the C emitter validates the whole crate envelope and
emits a guarded `int32_t` implementation. The driver finishes all semantic
work in memory and atomically publishes through a same-directory temporary.
Input/output inode aliases hard-error, and rejection preserves a preexisting
output.

The GCC and TinyCC executable gates require nonempty C and executable
artifacts, no output, and exit status 7. Exact-envelope tests also reject an
unsupported binary-expression body and an additional exported function
without changing a sentinel output. This is evidence for the first vertical
slice only.

The second bounded executable checkpoint adds typed named-local reads,
resolved free-function calls with ordered substitutions and arguments, exact
u32 monomorphized MIR instances, move operands, direct call terminators,
explicit public no-mangle roots, recursive callee-before-caller reachability,
and emission of every admitted reachable instance. Its C harness proves four
u32 values through a real emitted `identity::<u32>` function and `probe` call
under both GCC and TinyCC. Rejection tests cover unresolved, missing, and
unsupported substitutions plus unsupported exported roots; a private
unreachable unsupported function compiles but is absent from the artifact.
HIR and MIR dumps are `hir-v12` and `mir-v3`. This remains a deliberately
bounded identity/reachability slice: general expressions, control flow,
matches, generic multi-crate reachability, layout, and a full link driver are
still absent, so M5-01, M5-03, M5-05, and M5-07 remain `TODO`.

The third bounded executable checkpoint adds body-owned typed u32 literals and
recursive HIR add trees, explicit MIR `USE`/`BINARY` rvalues, distinct i32/u32
constant storage, and exact one- or two-argument u32 instances. The portable C
emitter now preserves a two-parameter public `add(left, right)` ABI and emits
real unsigned addition. GCC and TinyCC harnesses prove ordinary values,
`0x7fffffff + 1`, `UINT32_MAX + 1 == 0`, `UINT32_MAX + UINT32_MAX`, and a
nontrivial wrap. Unsupported operators, mismatched suffixes, and an unsupported
second exported root preserve a sentinel or publish no artifact; the same
unsupported private body is omitted. GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit
byte-identical arithmetic and identity C. The later nested checkpoint advances
the MIR schema to `mir-v4`; general operators, control flow, and the
corpus-wide milestone gates remain open, so no M5 task is closed by this
focused evidence.

The fourth bounded executable checkpoint lowers recursive u32-add trees in
deterministic postorder. Every non-root binary value owns one typed temporary
local assigned exactly once before use; the root alone assigns `_0`. MIR model
validation authenticates the complete statement tree against HIR and rejects
wrong ordering, destinations, operands, types, duplicate or unused temporary
storage, and cyclic HIR. The C emitter declares each temporary and emits the
validated assignments in MIR order with explicit `uint32_t` casts. The nested
canary `left + (1u32 + right)` requires a real `_3` temporary and two ordered
additions, and its executable harness covers ordinary and wrapping cases. A
reachable inner subtraction preserves a sentinel or publishes no artifact.
The complete strict GCC and TinyCC matrices and the GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrix
pass; GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit byte-identical nested C. Canonical
dumps are `hir-v12` and `mir-v4`. Other operators, non-u32 trees, general
statements/control flow, and all corpus-wide M5 gates remain open.

The fifth bounded executable checkpoint composes recursive arithmetic with
resolved reachability: `identity::<u32>(left + (1u32 + right))`. Source-backed
HIR transactionally owns the complete call-argument tree. MIR emits both
postorder argument temporaries in the call block, then passes the final
temporary to the already-published exact identity instance; the continuation
block returns the call result. Model validation consumes the exact HIR tree,
all temporary locals and statements, and the call operand together, rejecting
reordered, fabricated, missing, unused, cyclic, or wrong-callee shapes. The C
emitter preserves that order and performs the real call with the computed
temporary. The executable gate covers ordinary and wrapping cases, while an
inner subtraction preserves a sentinel or publishes no artifact. This remains
a bounded single-call/single-argument slice. The complete strict GCC, TinyCC,
and GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass, and GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit
byte-identical computed-call C. General expression nesting, control flow, and
the corpus-wide M5 gates remain open.

The sixth bounded executable checkpoint admits an ordinary monomorphic u32
callee with one or two parameters and zero substitutions. Calls preflight and
own each argument independently, lower recursive add trees left-to-right
through one shared postorder temporary sequence, retain every ordered MIR call
operand, and recursively publish the exact callee instance before its caller.
The canary `add_pair(left + 1u32, right + 2u32)` emits two distinct temporary
assignments followed by a real two-argument call. Its GCC and TinyCC harnesses
prove `left + right + 3` modulo 2^32. Swapped, missing, extra, mistyped,
misordered, cyclic, fabricated, or wrong-callee MIR shapes fail exact model
validation; an unsupported second argument preserves a sentinel or publishes
no artifact. The generic identity path and prior arithmetic canaries remain
supported. This is still a bounded call-expression slice; general statements,
control flow, methods, ABI/layout, multi-crate reachability, and all corpus-wide
M5 gates remain open.

The seventh bounded executable checkpoint composes call results within the
admitted u32 expression tree. HIR preflights literals, locals, additions, and
calls recursively, adopts every nested call slice in one allocation, and rolls
the whole body back on rejection or OOM. Driver reachability walks the complete
tree and lowers every callee before its caller. MIR preserves left-to-right
evaluation as a linear CFG: each call terminates its current block, a nested
result owns the next temporary, and evaluation resumes in the immediately
following block. Exact model validation authenticates the full HIR tree against
all statements, calls, destinations, instance keys, operands, targets, blocks,
and temporary uses. The canaries prove both
`add_pair(add_pair(left + 1u32, right + 2u32), left + 3u32)` and
`add_pair(left + 1u32, right + 2u32) + (left + 3u32)` execute as
`2 * left + right + 6` modulo 2^32. A reachable subtraction rejects the whole
crate while preserving a sentinel or publishing nothing. Full strict GCC,
TinyCC 0.9.27, and GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass; GCC- and TCC-built
compilers emit an identical 1,102-byte artifact with SHA-256
`3121d75d53590e428025b810cc83c1e794017eac13ff2fb9fe57f8492d232e23`.
General statements, control flow, methods, ABI/layout, multi-crate reachability,
and every corpus-wide M5 gate remain open.

The eighth bounded executable checkpoint adds corpus-driven immutable local
bindings. The pinned Rust 1.90 core/alloc cache contains 4,127 `let`
declarations across
1,750 recognized function bodies. Of 2,549 immutable identifier bindings with
an inferred type, initializer, and no `let`-else, 296 have an initializer whose
syntax is composed only of identifiers, integer literals, addition, free calls,
and parentheses. A stricter whole-body syntax proxy admits only two additional
core/alloc bodies when those lets are added to the current expression
vocabulary, before type or resolution checks. The slice is selected to build a
durable statement/local sequencing spine, not as an immediate corpus-coverage
claim. The first admitted boundary remains narrower and explicit:
top-level immutable identifier bindings with an exact written `u32` type,
required initializer, no shadowing, and the already authenticated u32
literal/local/add/direct-call expression tree. HIR owns a flat ordered
statement array rather than recursively nesting `let` expressions, and
preflights the complete block before transactionally extending the body local
table. Visible-prefix lookup prevents self and forward reads. MIR places user
locals after arguments and before generated temporaries, lowers each
initializer directly into its user-local destination, and publicly revalidates
the stored body by replaying every statement, call, destination, block,
temporary, and tail against HIR before C publication. Reachability walks let
initializers before the tail and keeps its current expression stable across
callee publication, which can reallocate HIR storage.

The executable canary evaluates a call-bearing initializer and later tail as
`2 * left + right + 6` modulo 2^32. A reachable subtraction in an initializer
preserves an existing sentinel and publishes nothing at a fresh path, while an
unsupported private unreachable body remains omitted. Full strict GCC,
TinyCC 0.9.27, and GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass. GCC- and TCC-built
compilers emit an identical 689-byte artifact with SHA-256
`944b2e203c93278c1f8d45b3f8632894d29807a1637e31590ff5b4fb0ae9299c`.
Canonical dumps are `hir-v15` and `mir-v6`. Mutation, destructuring, type
inference, shadowing, nested statement blocks, general control flow, and every
corpus-wide M5 gate remain open, so this checkpoint does not change a
milestone state.

The bounded aggregate MIR checkpoint represents a local or temporary place as
an exact type/span plus up to 16 authenticated DefId/ordinal projections.
Direct bodies evaluate aggregate initializers in source order, normalize the
stored rvalue to declaration order, retain actual nominal temporary types, and
flatten nested reads. Scalar i32/u32 fields use `COPY_PLACE`; aggregate-valued
fields use `MOVE_PLACE`. Stored MIR owns deep copies of every field and
projection slice and rejects malformed coverage, identities, types, spans, or
chains transactionally. Focused strict GCC, TinyCC 0.9.27, and GCC
ASan/UBSan/leak gates pass. Aggregate calls, projected writes, exported
aggregate ABI, and C emission remain open, so M5-01 is active but not complete.

The first aggregate C99 checkpoint integrates checked layout with reachable
MIR. It discovers the complete nominal closure before output, emits
dependency-first ordinal-named structs with C99 `sizeof`, `offsetof`, and
alignment proofs, lowers declaration-ordered aggregate rvalues to designated
compound literals, and emits flattened ordinal member reads. A nested,
reordered executable canary runs under strict GCC and TinyCC 0.9.27 and checks
deterministic output plus atomic rejection of aggregate exported ABI,
represented layout, and aggregate calls. GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit the
same 2,031-byte aggregate artifact with SHA-256
`5c5c5363db4d2f711304a1d8bf74a5ff1860eb85bbcde1521725b5e103e26faf`.
Exported signatures and calls remain scalar-u32, and projected writes remain
rejected, so M5-05 is active rather than complete.

The next corpus-driven arithmetic checkpoint adds exact wrapping u32
subtraction without broadening the integer or operator boundary. A syntax-only
body census over pinned Rust 1.90 `library` covers 334 files and 6,803 function
bodies; its test-pruned proxy covers 327 files and 5,674 bodies. Method calls
remain the largest unsupported family (7,803 occurrences across 3,335 proxy
bodies), followed by non-add binary operators (2,909 across 1,051), unsafe
blocks (1,667 across 1,372), `if` (1,529 across 880), and references (1,437
across 1,000). The non-add operator counts are `==` 435, `-` 433, `<` 319,
`>` 215, `&` 172, `&&` 172, `!=` 168, `*` 164, `<=` 152, `>=` 147, `/` 138,
`||` 108, `>>` 77, `|` 76, `<<` 64, `%` 54, and `^` 15. Subtraction was
selected first because it reuses the authenticated typed-u32 expression path
without pretending that bool or control-flow semantics exist.

HIR and MIR retain distinct add/subtract operators and validate their exact
correspondence; portable C emits `(uint32_t)(left - right)`. The executable
gate proves ordinary subtraction, underflow, both `UINT32_MAX` boundary
directions, deterministic emission, ordered let assignment, and atomic
rejection of multiplication and signed subtraction. The complete strict GCC
and TinyCC 0.9.27 matrices and the GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrix pass. GCC- and
TCC-built compilers emit an identical 349-byte artifact with SHA-256
`a17532521ea7b06e4f1cc3d8b94104c0b4f082c99b47c088e65ef4e75ed5d375`.
Every broader operator, bool/control-flow behavior, and corpus-wide M5 gate
remains open, so this checkpoint changes no milestone state.

The following aggregate-call checkpoint closes the narrow internal call gap
identified after the aggregate C99 slice. The Rust 1.90 library syntax proxy
contains 397 function tails that are struct expressions and 90 calls with a
direct struct-expression argument. Source-backed HIR now types each argument
of a one- or two-parameter monomorphic call from its exact signature. Each may
be u32 or a checked same-crate nongeneric named struct, including mixed
`(Outer, u32) -> u32` calls. Aggregate arguments require an internal Rust-ABI
callee; the existing generic identity path stays u32-only. Aggregate returns,
generic/cross-crate aggregate calls, and exported aggregate ABI still reject.

MIR materializes every aggregate argument before its call terminator, retains
exact `MOVE` or `MOVE_PLACE` operands, and validates the callee parameter type,
monomorphic instance, same-crate identity, projection chain, and scalar-u32
result by replaying HIR. Stored operands and projections are deeply owned;
corruption tests cover types, kinds, ordinals, and aggregate-return mismatch.
Portable C emits checked ordinal-named structs by value only in internal static
signatures while exported declarations remain scalar-u32-only. The executable
canary constructs a reordered nested aggregate, passes it with a separately
materialized u32 argument, reads a nested field in the callee, and observes the
real result under both compilers. The complete strict GCC/TinyCC 0.9.27 and GCC
ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass. GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit an identical
2,059-byte artifact with SHA-256
`e422dd6a827d93e5dd74d02a977bb8f860a8398ea1981f9f064d29f11f36c59e`.
Projected writes, aggregate returns/exports, broader call resolution, and every
corpus-wide M5 gate remain open, so M5-01 and M5-05 stay active.

The first conditional checkpoint adds the smallest corpus-backed value CFG.
The pinned Rust 1.90 library syntax proxy contains 1,529 `if` expressions in
880 test-pruned functions and 435 `==` operators in 329. Root equality-if
forms occur in 212 expressions across 175 functions; 115 expressions across
98 functions include `else`. A strict call-free current-shape proxy leaves
nine functions, with an upstream u32-aligned anchor at
`library/core/src/num/f32.rs:765`.

The admitted boundary is exactly root
`if left == right { then_u32 } else { else_u32 }`: operands are u32, both
statement-free block tails are u32, and each arm may use the authenticated
local/literal/add/subtract tree. HIR creates or reuses one canonical bool type
transactionally. MIR `mir-v7` stores equality as a distinct bool-producing
rvalue and replays one four-block switch/true/false/join diamond, including
every operand, destination, statement, edge, and span. Portable C materializes
the internal bool as exact `uint8_t` 0 or 1 and exposes no bool ABI. Nested
conditionals, calls in the condition or arms, if-let, else-if, missing else,
`!=`, signed or aggregate equality, bool parameters/returns, and every other
reachable graph reject atomically.

The executable canary covers ordinary equality, unequal subtraction,
equal-path overflow, and unequal-path underflow. The complete strict GCC,
TinyCC 0.9.27, and GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass. GCC- and TCC-built
compilers emit an identical 493-byte artifact with SHA-256
`e0dce3f5cd322c89f64787a28a307095de2f27d8c8791cc5f01233e81d9275d7`.
The corpus-wide M5 gates remain open, so M5-01 and M5-05 stay active.

The target-width scalar checkpoint adds the first exact `usize` execution
spine. A pinned Rust 1.90 library census finds 691 functions with exact
`usize` parameters, 246 with exact `usize` returns, and 13 explicit
`let name: usize` declarations. The smallest production body already matching
the bounded source shape is `const_min` at
`library/core/src/slice/rotate.rs:275`:

```rust
const fn const_min(left: usize, right: usize) -> usize {
    if right < left { right } else { left }
}
```

HIR remains target-neutral and owns exact decimal `usize` literals through
`UINT64_MAX`, exact parameters/returns and immutable lets, wrapping `+`/`-`,
one- or two-argument same-module monomorphic calls, exact `usize < usize`, and
the corresponding root value diamond. MIR is target-qualified before
publication with an immutable 32- or 64-bit pointer width, stores `usize`
constants in `uint64_t`, rejects 32-bit overflow, and advances the canonical
dump to `mir-v9`. The legacy width-zero MIR path remains valid only for graphs
with no reachable `usize`; generic identity substitution remains u32-only.

Canonical callable instance format v2 and MIR `mir-v9` separately retain the
selected dispatch/symbol definition and `body_definition`, the definition
whose signature and HIR body execute. Semantic results, admission, reachable
MIR, lowering, dumps, and C emission authenticate both identities. Qualified
receiver-free calls can now inherit a nongeneric trait default through a
concrete local impl. Such an instance deliberately keeps
`selected_callable == body_definition == declared_trait_callable`: the
concrete `selected_impl`, `self_owner`, and `self_type` supply dispatch
identity, and the complete canonical key therefore gives two impls sharing
one default body distinct MIR and C symbols. No synthetic impl-method DefId is
created.

Portable C maps Rust `usize` to `uintptr_t`, emits explicit
`(uintptr_t)UINT32_C(...)` or `(uintptr_t)UINT64_C(...)` constants, and adds
compile-time `UINTPTR_MAX` and `sizeof(uintptr_t)` target proofs. The executable
canary combines the production `const_min` diamond, explicit lets, wrapping
subtraction/addition, two real internal calls, and `4294967301usize`. Atomic
negative gates cover i686 overflow, mixed u32/usize ABI, aggregate source-call
boundaries, context-free or malformed literals, generic usize substitution,
and target/MIR/host width mismatch. The complete strict GCC, TinyCC 0.9.27, and
GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass. GCC- and TCC-built compilers emit identical
artifacts for both target widths: the x86-64 executable canary is 1,277 bytes
with SHA-256
`0260d79f545b2b2646f9f458166e04ead626e9cae78ca8da2e60f14e3e09b90f`;
the width-only fixtures are 869 bytes on x86-64
(`ff03a288fd5dee04948f0834877d2321089d870e132647482181194f59bb6f8d`)
and 737 bytes on i686
(`599b085f5cff06ba1b1beb4f67d9b2619f3ba10ff3e2286f7f31762024a7e9f4`).
Inference, mixed-width operations, methods, casts, division, shifts, bool ABI,
aggregate usize source calls, and every corpus-wide M5 gate remain open, so
M5-01, M5-04, and M5-05 stay active.

The authenticated Rust 1.90 `library/core` module graph now completes with
293 loaded source files, 378 modules, and zero errors under the empty cfg gate.
Context-aware impl-member reparsing clears both
`zip_impl_general_defaults!` calls; parser-validated `ty` fragment boundaries
and transactional repetition-index rollback clear the adjacent generic types
generated by `integer_sum_product!`. A deliberately bounded parameter-style
declarative path admits only private, same-AST, definition-before-use,
unqualified parenthesized source invocations by translating their stored
parameter/body pair into one rules arm. This clears `impl_atomic_primitive!`
without claiming imported, public, qualified, forward, generated, or
cross-module Macro 2.0 scope and hygiene. Bounded `${concat(...)}`,
`${ignore(...)}`, `${index()}`, `${count(...)}`, and same-crate `$crate`
transcription clear the later `fmt`, tuple, and portable-SIMD graph gates.
Trait-associated macro invocations and full associated-item grammar validation
remain unsupported. This is graph construction, not HIR lowering, type
checking, library metadata, or executable core support.

The canonical x86_64 target-cfg graph separately loads all 363 reachable Rust
1.90 `core` sources and constructs 451 modules without an error.
Outer attributes on `let` statements retain ordered statement-owned IDs and
spans instead of being misparsed as let-condition expressions; block-local
`static` declarations are ordinary item statements and never enter module
namespaces or effective roots. HIR body lowering validates attributed-let
storage and rejects it until statement-attribute semantics exist. Bare cfg
predicates test key presence, so value-bearing target atomic cfg facts also
activate their guarded macro declarations. This is a successful
target-configured graph, not a HIR, type-checking, library metadata, or
executable core claim.

`extern crate self as core` now publishes the alias, rather than the reserved
source name, as a type-namespace binding to the crate-root module. The import
resolver exposes that binding through its extern-prelude fallback in every
module. This clears 49 target-configured core import failures. A graph-owned
cfg-filtered enum variant view then clears all 38 remaining unresolved
imports; the exact frontier is now 30 errors: 0 unresolved, 13 ambiguous, and
17 cyclic. HIR still rejects before mutation while any import diagnostic
remains.

Enum variants retain ordered outer attribute IDs and exact spans instead of
discarding attributes during parsing. The module graph applies the same
cfg/cfg_attr processor used by items and publishes only active variants, with
their exact enum declaration and original zero-based AST index. Enum self,
explicit variant, alias, glob, and checked-path lookup preserve that identity;
all variants occupy the type namespace, while unit and tuple constructors also
occupy the value namespace. HIR rejects variant import bindings until its
canonical import schema gains a distinct variant target instead of pointing at
the parent enum definition.

The authenticated Rust 1.90 `library/alloc` empty-cfg graph now completes with
all 62 source files, 67 modules, and zero errors when supplied the successful
core dependency artifact. Generic parameters retain ordered outer attributes
and exact declaration provenance, clearing `#[may_dangle]` on `RawVec`'s
`Drop` impl; HIR rejects those attributes explicitly until their semantics
exist. Unicode character literals distinguish a bounded UTF-8 scalar such as
`'Σ'` from a Unicode lifetime without embedding Unicode tables in the
TinyCC-compatible lexer. All twelve `core::bstr` imports now resolve through
exact consumer use leaves. Nested zero-width repetition choices backtrack
without becoming global syntax errors, a consumed trailing separator counts
as progress, and type-position macro invocations retain their path, delimiter,
arguments, and span. Those last two syntax fixes clear alloc's generated
`to_string_str!` implementations without pretending type macros are already
expanded for HIR.

The first cross-crate hygiene substrate retains `$crate` as a distinct
transcriber node and accepts only one explicit ASCII crate identifier from an
exact resolver-certified binding. Planner bindings carry that borrowed
defining-crate identity through recursive generated invocations, so an
imported definition using `$crate` cannot silently target the consuming crate.
Local and legacy-null bindings remain exactly `crate`; path-like guesses such
as `core::guessed` reject.

A live dependency-macro artifact binds one successful dependency graph, its
exact revision, an extern name, and a distinct `$crate` identifier. Consumer
graph options accept a bounded artifact array only for the synchronous build.
Each unqualified source invocation must have one exact unresolved external
use leaf; local collisions, dependency-rooted globs, private paths, competing
leaves/artifacts, stale/null/self artifacts, and duplicate extern or hygienic
crate identifiers reject. Unrelated globs do not block an otherwise exact
certificate. Source-written hygienic paths remain unauthorized.

The planner uses graph-local 64-bit owners above the source-ID range for
dependency ASTs. Generated-only lookup asks the registered artifact on demand
for the authenticated hygienic path, so an imported outer macro can emit a
different `$crate::module::helper!` without enumerating guessed public macros.
Private and ambiguous generated paths retain fail-closed classifications.
When final effective items are published, high owners become a separate
dependency reference; dependency source IDs never enter the consumer's local
macro-definition field.

That reference carries the exact consumer graph/generation, a per-definition
certificate, the consumer-local dependency ID, dependency revision, and
dependency-scoped declaration. A revision-checked accessor validates only
definitions present in the final published registry, not abandoned staging
rounds. The result is a consumer-owned snapshot, so validation does not require
the dependency graph or temporary artifact to remain live. Field splices,
cross-graph references, prior consumer generations, failed rebuilds, and
uncertified declarations reject. The graph also retains the exact consumer
module, source-qualified use declaration, local alias, invocation AST owner,
and producing dependency definition for each published external expansion.
HIR may therefore treat only that leaf's otherwise-unresolved macro import as
consumed; grouped sibling errors and unrelated imports still reject before
mutation. Supported body-free declarations and authenticated root consts
generated into the consumer AST now lower after both the import leaf and
dependency certificate validate, even after the producer graph and artifact
are destroyed. No-op dependency macros remain conservatively unresolved
because they publish no generated syntax.

The first HIR library artifact snapshots one successfully lowered producer's
public module/type namespace from its exact graph revision and HIR module map.
It copies public module edges plus same-crate traits, ADTs, type aliases,
extern types, and type-namespace reexports; producer graph, AST, import
resolver, and map lifetimes therefore do not leak into consumers. The shared
HIR context remains the owner of producer DefIds and must stay live. A
consumer lowering call may borrow multiple artifacts with unique extern names
and resolve explicit paths such as `dep::api::Type` or `dep::Trait`; local
definitions win, producer aliases normalize through their loaded HIR, and
private paths or duplicate extern identities reject transactionally. One exact
unresolved, non-glob external type-namespace leaf may be authenticated against
an artifact and retained as a structural HIR binding to a producer type,
trait, or module DefId. Aliases and grouped leaves preserve source order, and
a qualified path may continue below an imported module alias. Producer-owned
traits now work in impl headers, supertraits and generic bounds, associated
bounds, and qualified projections; generic signatures and associated
declarations are read from bound producer HIR rather than fabricated local
records. A private sibling, local collision, competing leaf/artifact, glob, or
unrelated resolver error rejects the whole transaction. Values, external
globs, transitive external reexports, standalone/serialized metadata, and
executable alloc remain the next library-artifact boundary.

M3-04 now has transaction, restoration, and format foundations plus one
narrow semantic round trip. Public
HIR-context marks capture every append-only model vector plus its interner and
arena;
rewind releases expression-owned heap storage before truncation, commit keeps
the appended graph, and resolving an outer mark safely invalidates nested
marks. A graph-independent owned namespace representation validates a supplied
same-context crate/root and every module/entry DefId, then moves into an
artifact only after all checks; failed replacement preserves both the prior
artifact and the exact candidate allocations. The initial `cmhir-meta-v1`
codec now emits and validates a deterministic 40-byte versioned envelope,
64-MiB-bounded little-endian section frames, and IEEE CRC32 without zlib.
The semantic layer now uses six fixed-order sections: `CRAT`, `MODS`, `GPAR`,
`TYPE`, `ITEM`, and `NSPC`. It encodes crate name/edition, a root-connected
module tree, public extern types, structs, unions, enums and variants, free
type aliases, owner-grouped lifetime/type generics and type defaults,
supported structural types and scalar constants, module/type aliases and
reexports, and builtin primitive bindings. One-based
file-local module/item handles replace runtime IDs; pointers, intern IDs,
DefIds, and source IDs never enter the file. Decode parses and validates the
complete wire payload before mutation, creates fresh runtime identities under
a `CmHirContextMark`, restores owner-contiguous generics/defaults and
child-first structural types, binds ADT/alias payloads, and restores the
namespace over remapped DefIds before commit and atomic artifact replacement.
Failure destroys the candidate and rewinds the context. Tests cover a producer
runtime
crate ID of two loading as consumer ID one, producer destruction before load,
a zero-item primitive-only artifact, CRC and truncation damage, and a valid-CRC
namespace conflict that reaches restoration before proving exact
context/artifact rollback. Encoding now canonicalizes modules by full
declaration path, items by owner path and declared name, and namespace
entries by owner path, exported name, wire kind, and canonical target,
remapping all one-based handles after sorting. Two fresh producer processes
deliberately reverse module/item creation and owned module/namespace insertion
yet write byte-identical nonempty files; a fresh prepopulated consumer then
proves nonzero runtime-ID remapping and lookup.

The expanded fixture now starts from parsed Rust and runs through the real
module graph, import resolver, HIR lowerer, public library-artifact capture,
and metadata encoder. It round-trips `Wrapper<T = u32>`, generic enum
`Choice<T>`, a generic union, lifetime-bearing references, structural tuple,
pointer, and literal-array types, `Alias = Wrapper<u32>`, fields/variants,
primitives, and child-module reexports. Forward/reverse parsed producers remain
byte-identical after source-side graph/import state is destroyed; a fresh
prepopulated consumer proves ID remapping, alias normalization, and exact
rollback for prevalidation and post-mark semantic corruption. Lifetime generic
names such as `'a` are validated without broadening item or module identifiers.
Traits, associated declarations, projections, impls, bodies, const-generic
arguments/defaults, unevaluated constants, and dependency-backed type edges
remain mandatory
before M3-04's acceptance is met, so M3-04 stays `ACTIVE`. The private driver
now provides `--emit-cmhir SOURCE --crate-name NAME -o FILE` and repeated
`--extern-cmhir NAME FILE`. Separate processes prove parsed-source production,
atomic publication, fresh-ID dependent loading, external ADT resolution, and
dependent republication. These are still raw `cmhir` declarations rather than
`.rmeta`/`.rlib`, object/archive production, or link orchestration. Values,
macros, native links, bodies, and dependency graphs remain later work. The
complete strict GCC and TinyCC
matrices plus a focused GCC ASan/UBSan/leak run pass with this narrow layer
linked into the compiler.

The earlier `pub macro assert_matches` syntax gate in `macros/mod.rs` is also
cleared by opaque, declaration-preserving parsing. Later
front-end/library-artifact gates include HIR handling for the retained
self-crate alias plus real `.rmeta`/`.rlib` production. Once source
reachability is honest, the largest measured backend lever is unsuffixed
integers (18,102 occurrences across 5,605 pinned library bodies). The first
exact contextual slice now handles bare decimal `i32`, `u32`, and `usize`
literals in exact return, explicit-let, aggregate-field, branch/operator, and
direct-call contexts. It deliberately does not implement inference variables,
unification, or fallback defaulting. Implicit unit/zero-argument calls and named
aggregate returns remain subsequent high-frequency levers. None of those
counts changes a milestone state.

## M4: Types and semantic lowering

| ID | State | Task | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| M4-01 | ACTIVE | Type variables and constraint solver | Focused inference corpus passes |
| M4-02 | ACTIVE | Trait/impl and associated-type selection | Selected impl dump matches oracle |
| M4-03 | TODO | Coercions, unsizing, const generics/eval | Core/alloc semantic corpus passes |
| M4-04 | TODO | Closures, async/generators, vtables | Layout and call behavior matches oracle |
| M4-05 | TODO | Conservative lifetime/borrow policy | Bootstrap corpus accepted; no runtime erasures |

The semantic implementation order is fixed so declaration progress cannot
bypass proof obligations: immutable parameter environments; canonical,
binder-aware tabled goals; transactional generic impl replay; recursive
predicate evaluation; projection normalization; authenticated closed-universe
metadata; body constraint generation and adjustments; post-typecheck rewrites;
const evaluation; and conservative region/borrow acceptance. Solver results use
the explicit lattice `PROVEN`, `NEGATIVE`, `NO_SOLUTION`, `AMBIGUOUS`,
`DEFERRED_INFERENCE`, `DEFERRED_METADATA`, `UNSUPPORTED`, and `OVERFLOW`.
Unknown facts, ordinary cycles, unsupported candidates, and incomplete metadata
never become success. Only an explicitly modeled coinductive auto-trait cycle
may use coinduction, and negative candidates are considered before structural
auto-trait recursion.

The semantic-pass foundation now reaches its first source-facing inference
slice without advancing an M4 state. Type checking can transactionally bind
every still-unresolved canonical
integer variable to an exact HIR default such as `i32`, while leaving already
constrained roots and general variables untouched. Projection normalization
can publish an ordered, all-or-nothing trace containing the normalized input
projection, the proof-selected raw target, the recursively normalized target,
and the exact parameter-environment or impl/associated-item proof locator. The
semantic-session wrapper authenticates the same session and substitution
before exposing that trace; failed, stale, cyclic, or fuel-limited attempts
expose no partial evidence. Successful nonempty traces retain their exact
scratch-term owner, a process-unique owner lifetime, and the scratch-state
revision at publication. Rollback, variable rebinding, or destroy/reinitialize
therefore prevents coincident numeric IDs from becoming evidence for unrelated
terms. Production expression-type normalization now synchronously stages each
nonempty trace at the exact final, non-speculative decision that required it.
Semantic results durably deep-copy, group, rebase, seal, and query those records
for ordinary bodies and exact instances, keyed by body, expression, decision
kind, and decision index. Seal validation authenticates expression ownership,
unique keys, complete disjoint step ranges, every type endpoint and proof
locator, and equality between the trace result and the published unadjusted
expression type. Failed normalization, stale or foreign scratch evidence,
duplicate decisions, later checked-body failure, and transaction rollback
publish no partial trace. MIR does not yet consume projection traces, so they
are causal semantic evidence rather than an executable projection recipe.

MIR now has fail-closed schema support for tagged field/dereference place
projections and shared-borrow rvalues over validated places. Dereference steps
require a reference input and a canonical empty payload; shared borrows require
an erased immutable reference target whose pointee exactly matches the source
place. Storage sizing, deep copy, equality, replay scratch, and dumps preserve
the tags. HIR now has explicit body-owned shared-borrow and built-in-dereference
expressions with place, type, region, span, and ownership validation. Semantic
admission, reachable discovery, and semantic-results publication reject both
nodes explicitly until exact adjustment/place-use recipes exist; source body
lowering also remains closed. The C backend is ready for immutable erased
references: it emits const-pointer declarators, parenthesized tagged
dereference places, and shared-address rvalues while rejecting mutable, raw,
non-erased, and trait-deref forms. Authenticated HIR-to-MIR matching still has
no borrow/dereference cases. Focused MIR tests construct valid typed reference
HIR and prove that both a shared borrow and a borrow-then-dereference reject
transactionally without increasing the MIR body count. Aggregate reference
layout remains open, so this is representation/backend readiness rather than
executable source borrow support.

M4-01 now has a session-owned scratch type DAG for primitives, references,
pointers, tuples, arrays, slices, function pointers, ADTs, rigid parameters,
and projections. General, integer, and float inference variables use
rank-balanced union-find with deterministic canonical IDs, transactional undo
snapshots, iterative occurs checks, shared-pair structural unification, atomic
failure rollback, cached HIR import, and solved-only HIR freeze. HIR rewinds or
context reinitialization invalidate stale sessions, recursive import/unify/
freeze depth fails closed, and region identity is retained for output while
equality is deferred to later region obligations. Focused tests cover class
conflicts, rollback, deep/shared DAGs, stale HIR, and freeze atomicity; the
complete strict GCC, TinyCC, and GCC ASan/UBSan/leak matrices pass. Exact-owner
instantiation substitutes lifetime, type, and const arguments plus a separately
authenticated trait/impl `Self`; foreign parameters remain rigid, const values
are checked against their declared parameter types, shared HIR DAGs retain
sharing, and every mismatch or overflow rolls back atomically. The first
production body constraint slice assigns private general terms to immutable
unannotated simple lets, assigns integer terms to unsuffixed decimal literals,
imports exact suffixed and local types, and recursively propagates equality
through prior-local references plus primitive addition/subtraction trees and an
exact tail-return context. Arithmetic inference is limited to the existing
wrapping `u32`/`usize` slice when a concrete HIR type is already available;
literal/local-only roots may still default to `i32`, while signed fallback
arithmetic remains closed until overflow semantics are modeled. Only solved
integer kinds are staged before concrete HIR publication.
Scratch IDs never escape. Rejection performs no HIR rewind or semantic
mutation, preserving both HIR generations as well as every body, local, type,
expression, interner, and arena snapshot; the crate-wide outer mark remains
authoritative across multiple bodies. Calls, operators, branches,
aggregates, general unresolved variables, coercions, const inference, trait
obligations, associated-type normalization, method lookup, and diagnostics
still need full draft-based constraint generation, so M4-01 remains `ACTIVE`.

M4-02 now has an immutable deterministic impl-head index and a transactional
selector for positive ordinary impls. Type-only impl generics are instantiated
with fresh inference variables;
candidate trials roll back and a unique winner is recreated and committed.
Repeated and nested constrained parameters are supported, while unused,
lifetime, const, defaulted, or otherwise unauthenticated impl parameters block
explicitly. Stale or cross-HIR sessions reject, malformed named terms and named
regions do not become proofs, and shared DAG depth is height-checked. A narrow
predicate slice instantiates required, binder-free implemented-trait predicates
under the candidate's exact impl-owner substitution and solves them recursively
through the same canonical table. Candidate and predicate trials roll back;
ordinary re-entry is ambiguous, cycle-tainted non-proofs are not cached, and an
unproved predicate blocks an otherwise structural winner. Auto traits, negative
impls, projection predicates, higher-ranked/outlives predicates, and other
unsupported forms block rather than being skipped. The metadata universe is
deliberately open: absence always
returns `DEFERRED_METADATA`, because HIR and `cmhir-meta-v1` do not yet provide
an authenticated complete impl universe. Immutable owner-authenticated
parameter environments now collect exact and enclosing predicates, outlives
facts, trait `Self`, supertraits, and positive impl headers from one exact HIR
snapshot. Facts materialize transactionally under separate exact/enclosing
substitutions; higher-ranked, projection, modifier, outlives, recursive,
mixed-owner, foreign-owner, and overflow cases remain explicit pending or
blocking outcomes. Environment assumptions are tried before the impl index and
a winning proof is replayed atomically. The first immutable goal table keys
implemented-trait goals structurally across the goal, binder universe/de Bruijn
identity, and exact/enclosing substitutions. Type and lifetime inference
variables are alpha-canonicalized by first occurrence; iterative tri-color DAG
walking bounds depth/nodes, rejects malformed cycles, and makes shared versus
duplicated structure equivalent. Deterministic non-proof outcomes may be
cached, ordinary evaluating re-entry is ambiguity rather than proof, and
PROVEN is deliberately replayed through the transactional solver. A
generation-bound semantic session now composes one index, exact/enclosing
parameter environment, canonical table, and type-check context. The bounded C
emission path publishes its complete reachable HIR closure, creates one such
session per caller owner, and proves every supported generic call predicate
before constructing any MIR; every non-proof blocks output atomically.
Projection normalization, broader predicate forms, semantic metadata, and
general body-constraint integration remain required, so M4-02 stays `ACTIVE`.

A whole-local-crate semantic-item barrier now runs after complete reachable HIR
publication and before per-owner semantic sessions or MIR. For supported local
positive impls it authenticates trait and associated-item DefIds, instantiates
concrete trait type arguments plus `Self`, requires targetless associated types
and required methods, permits omitted default methods, and checks supplied
method receiver/arity/types/return/ABI/safety/const/async/variadic shape. Rigid
or foreign parameters cannot enter an impl header, associated target, or
nongeneric impl method. Generic impls, GATs, generic methods, higher-ranked,
outlives, predicate-bearing, associated-bound, projection,
associated-type-default, negative, auto-trait, cross-crate, and unsupported
associated-item shapes block with an explicit status; specialization remains
an explicit earlier HIR-lowering rejection. Focused strict GCC, TinyCC,
ASan/UBSan/leak, and end-to-end atomic-output tests pass, as do the complete
strict GCC and TinyCC suites. This closes only the supported item-conformance
slice; associated consts, generic impl/method conformance, projection
normalization, complete metadata, and general body type checking keep M4-02
`ACTIVE`.

Canonical projection-equality goals now share the implemented-trait goal
table. Their keys include both projection and expected terms; proof requires a
unique recursively proven impl, an authenticated impl-associated definition,
and target instantiation under the replayed substitution. Root projection
targets normalize recursively through the same table. Every non-proof rolls
back scratch state and evidence, overlap remains ambiguous even when only one
target matches, and a unique target mismatch is `NO_SOLUTION`. GATs, nested
structural projections, associated defaults, specialization, and incomplete
foreign metadata remain distinct fail-closed outcomes. The next body-semantic
integration must retain durable projection-proof recipes and broaden their
source-expression use. Callee predicate checking already constructs and
dispatches projection-equality goals through the semantic session; it no
longer rejects them or treats them as implemented-trait facts.

The next body-typing milestone generalizes this bounded inferred-let draft
instead of adding another reachable-expression special case. It is an
all-local, cfg-active transactional typed-HIR barrier that runs
before reachability and MIR. Its dependency sequence is general constraint
generation/finalization; path, trait, and projection lookup; coercions and
immutable adjustments; methods/operators/callables; patterns and binding
modes; closures/coroutines; post-typecheck rewrites; region/outlives and
conservative borrowing; then CTFE and broad validated MIR. No later pass may
consume partially typed local HIR or reinterpret an unsupported semantic state
as proof.

Production now has the first bounded whole-crate ordering barrier. The C
driver invokes transactional `cm_semantic_admit_local_crate` immediately after
module-graph HIR lowering and before legacy entry or exported-root discovery.
Every cfg-active supported free-function or concrete trait-impl method body is
therefore lowered and definition-checked, and an invalid unreachable body
rejects atomically before MIR or output publication. Original mrustc completes
outer and expression type checking, usage
annotation, lifetime/reborrow, and validation passes before translation
enumeration and monomorphization. The next production barrier must invoke the
remaining semantic passes in that same causal position. The driver now retains
the all-local admission capability through root discovery, reachability, exact
admission, MIR construction, validation, and commit. Reachability is read-only:
it carries no module/import lowering state, never invokes body lowering, and
requires current all-local semantic evidence for every discovered body before
it can enter the exact-instance closure. Cfg-disabled invalid bodies are removed
before this barrier and neither poison admission nor enter emitted reachability.
The current semantic
metadata artifact contains declarations plus trait/impl universe, not typed
bodies or semantic recipes; it must not be described as this barrier until a
versioned durable body/results schema and completeness manifest exist.

Semantic results now publish immutable, canonical per-expression adjustment
sequences plus exact primitive-binary and named-field selection recipes.
Ordinary-body and exact-instance MIR lowering consume those recipes, and MIR
replay authenticates the same expression identities, operator or aggregate
selection, field ordinal, and operand/result types. Adjustments are represented
and queried in order, but current HIR and MIR have no executable borrow,
deref, unsize, reification, or other adjustment forms; every nonzero sequence
therefore remains fail-closed instead of being rediscovered from endpoint
types. Focused tests cover ordinary and exact-instance recipe queries,
malformed or duplicate primitive and field facts, malformed and noncontiguous
adjustment facts, wrong instance identities, and publication of no stage state
after every rejected writeback. This establishes the authenticated evidence
boundary only; coercion generation and executable adjustment lowering remain
open under M4-03 and the all-local typed-HIR sequence above.

Exact free-function closures, including recursive strongly connected
components, now cross the admitted HIR-to-MIR
boundary with type-only item arguments and the currently supported proven
callee predicates. Admission derives every
callee's canonical identity from the authenticated caller instance and the HIR
call substitutions, requires both endpoints in the closed set, rejects
duplicate or omitted edges atomically, and seals cross-instance return and
parameter types. Exact direct-call queries are consumed by both MIR lowering
and replay; an unselected or phantom substitution cannot fall back to a
definition-level `DefId`. Semantic type-view authentication recognizes each
sealed instance-owned byte arena. This remains a bounded bridge: stored MIR
identity is still `DefId + CmHirTypeId[]`, the driver materializes only its
existing optional-u32 substitution subset. MIR publication reserves stable
instance IDs, defines and validates the complete overlay, and commits all
bodies atomically; incomplete reservations never become visible through the
public MIR context. Transactions pin process-unique MIR-context lifetime and
semantic-admission capability identities, so destroy/reinitialize ABA cannot
re-authorize a draft. Compile-only self and mutual recursion pass, while useful
terminating recursion still needs calls nested in general conditional control
flow. M4 remains active until the all-local semantic barrier and the pass
sequence above replace these bounded slices.

## M5: MIR, monomorphization, and TCC C backend

| ID | State | Task | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| M5-01 | ACTIVE | HIR-to-MIR including matches | Unoptimized MIR matches normalized oracle dump |
| M5-02 | TODO | MIR cleanup and required intrinsics | Existing MIR tests pass; optimizer may be off |
